From the doorway, a dragon‑shaped creature slides onto a high bar stool, scales glittering like polished obsidian. Its wings unfurl to reveal a pair of amber eyes that scan the room. The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

<<<PROMPT_HISTORY_CONTENT 9 LENGTH 2421>>>
STATIC SETTING FACTS
Medieval tavern interior featuring a creaking wooden floor, a central counter adjacent to a hearth that holds a barrel, a back table, a high bar stool with shelves of crystal cups, lanterns positioned at each table for persistent lighting, and a wooden latch on the back door

SPATIAL REFINEMENT
The tavern occupies a rectangular hall that measures **30 ft east‑west by 20 ft north‑south** and is laid out on a creaking wooden floor.

### Anchor objects (large or fixed)

1. **Back door with wooden latch**  
   - Located at the **western wall**, centered along its length.  
   - Door width: **4 ft**; height: **7 ft**.  

2. **Hearth and barrel**  
   - Hearth sits **10 ft north of the southern wall** (the centre of the north‑south axis).  
   - Hearth dimensions: **8 ft east‑west × 6 ft deep**, standing on the floor.  
   - Barrel rests in front of the hearth, facing south; diameter **3 ft**, height **5 ft**.

### Counter and seating

3. **Central counter**  
   - Runs parallel to the western wall, positioned just east of the hearth.  
   - Length: **12 ft north‑south** (from 4 ft west of the eastern wall down to 2 ft from the southern wall).  
   - Depth into room: **2 ft**.  

4. **High bar stools with crystal cup shelves**  
   - Four stools placed along the counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway.  
   - Stool height (to seat): **7 ft**; depth of stool platform: **1 ft × 1 ft**.  
   - Shelves above stools run east‑west, 2 ft deep by 6 ft long, holding crystal cups.

5. **Back table**  
   - Located just north of the back door on the western wall.  
   - Table dimensions: **4 ft east‑west × 3 ft deep**, with a 1 ft depth for chairs.  

### Lighting

6. **Lanterns at each table**  
   - Small lanterns hung from the ceiling above every seating area (counter stools and back table).  
   - Lantern diameter: **0.8 ft**, height: **1 ft**; positioned 5 ft above floor level.

All objects are spaced to avoid overlap: the hearth’s depth leaves a clear 4‑ft aisle northward, the counter sits east of that space, and the back table is directly adjacent to the western door. The high bar stools sit on the counter with ample room between them for patrons, while lanterns provide continuous illumination without blocking any pathways.

Return all three required parts: Location line, valid double-quoted JSON, then plain-text description based on that JSON.

You are the creative director for one 8-second video segment.

JOB
- CURRENT BEAT is the story authority for what happens now and the scene to stage in this clip.
- PREVIOUS SHOT END is frame-0 context for continuation shots. Start there without replaying it; if it conflicts with CURRENT BEAT, CURRENT BEAT wins.
- CANONICAL STARTING CHARACTER FACTS, when provided, are authoritative identity/appearance facts. Establish applicable visible facts for characters already present in CURRENT BEAT or ASSIGNED SOURCE; never introduce a character only to show a canonical fact.

WRITE THE SCENE
- Show CURRENT BEAT clearly with concrete visible/audible action.
- Complete every finite action explicitly assigned by CURRENT BEAT, including the required result for every named person or target, before the final continuity state.
- Add only details needed to physically connect or clearly show CURRENT BEAT.
- Preserve spatial continuity. If an actor must interact with something at another established position, show the actor moving there first; do not use impossible reach, teleportation, or a hidden cut.
- Preserve body support/elevation literally. Do not put a subject onto, over, across, or down from a counter, table, bar, shelf, stool, or other support unless the prior state establishes that route/position or CURRENT BEAT requires it. Prefer ordinary floor routes over gratuitous climbing. A subject may not climb furniture merely to reach an ordinary prop, perform routine work, or add visual business when a floor-level route or reachable surface is available.
- Do not use "appears", "suddenly appears", "pops into view", or equivalent wording as a substitute for entry/reveal staging.
- Preserve prop identity.

CAMERA CHOREOGRAPHY
- Stage the entire segment as one continuous camera take. Do not use cuts unless CURRENT BEAT explicitly requires a location change that cannot be shown continuously.
- When framing, angle, distance, height, or viewed subject must change, move the camera instead of cutting. Use natural English camera actions such as pushes in, pulls out, pans left/right, trucks left/right, tilts up/down, pedestals up/down, arcs around, or tracks with a moving subject. A static shot is valid when movement would not help.
- Camera movement must follow, reveal, or refocus important story action.
- CONTINUATION: the supplied opening guide is the visual authority for frame 0. PREVIOUS SHOT END is semantic state, not a camera shot list. Describe only the inherited continuation frame before CURRENT BEAT; do not reconstruct every known subject into view.

You are the creative director for one 8-second video segment.

JOB
- CURRENT BEAT is the story authority for what happens now and the scene to stage in this clip.
- PREVIOUS SHOT END is frame-0 context for continuation shots. Start there without replaying it; if it conflicts with CURRENT BEAT, CURRENT BEAT wins.
- CANONICAL STARTING CHARACTER FACTS, when provided, are authoritative identity/appearance facts. Establish applicable visible facts for characters already present in CURRENT BEAT or ASSIGNED SOURCE; never introduce a character only to show a canonical fact.

WRITE THE SCENE
- Show CURRENT BEAT clearly with concrete visible/audible action.
- Complete every finite action explicitly assigned by CURRENT BEAT, including the required result for every named person or target, before the final continuity state.
- Add only details needed to physically connect or clearly show CURRENT BEAT.
- Preserve spatial continuity. If an actor must interact with something at another established position, show the actor moving there first; do not use impossible reach, teleportation, or a hidden cut.
- Preserve body support/elevation literally. Do not put a subject onto, over, across, or down from a counter, table, bar, shelf, stool, or other support unless the prior state establishes that route/position or CURRENT BEAT requires it. Prefer ordinary floor routes over gratuitous climbing. A subject may not climb furniture merely to reach an ordinary prop, perform routine work, or add visual business when a floor-level route or reachable surface is available.
- Do not use "appears", "suddenly appears", "pops into view", or equivalent wording as a substitute for entry/reveal staging.
- Preserve prop identity.

CAMERA CHOREOGRAPHY
- Stage the entire segment as one continuous camera take. Do not use cuts unless CURRENT BEAT explicitly requires a location change that cannot be shown continuously.
- When framing, angle, distance, height, or viewed subject must change, move the camera instead of cutting. Use natural English camera actions such as pushes in, pulls out, pans left/right, trucks left/right, tilts up/down, pedestals up/down, arcs around, or tracks with a moving subject. A static shot is valid when movement would not help.
- Camera movement must follow, reveal, or refocus important story action.
- CONTINUATION: the supplied opening guide is the visual authority for frame 0. PREVIOUS SHOT END is semantic state, not a camera shot list. Describe only the inherited continuation frame before CURRENT BEAT; do not reconstruct every known subject into view.
- Keep all timed action inside the 8-second clip.
- Spread CURRENT BEAT naturally across the clip with at least 4 timed micro-beats, with the final timed micro-beat in the final quarter.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.

Finally, Amy turns off the lanterns at each table and pulls a wooden latch on the back door. She steps outside to close it, locking the tavern for the night as the moon rises over the medieval town.

North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

- Preserve prop identity.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

RAW SCENE
At 00:00.000, Goblin_1 steps onto the counter from the east side of the room, chin resting on its edge; he holds a chipped mug in one hand.  
At 00:00.250, the camera is positioned east of the central counter, a slight angle looking west toward the hearth and back table. The focus is on a green‑skinned goblin leaning over the counter with his chin resting on its edge; he holds a chipped mug in one hand.

Amy stands behind him, her left foot near the counter’s edge, eyes fixed on the barrel beside the hearth. She lifts the ale‑scented cloth from her palm, places it to the side of the polished table, and releases it onto the floor beside the table as she looks at the finish—this is the inherited position from the previous beat.

At 00:01.500, the camera pans left a few feet, pulling both subjects into frame. The goblin’s right hand still grips the mug; Amy’s right arm extends toward the barrel’s lid.

At 00:02.750, Amy lifts her arm and tilts the barrel slightly, tipping liquid out of its side. A stream of ale pours from the barrel onto the chipped mug.

At 00:04.250, the liquid flows steadily; the mug fills up to near its brim, the surface catching the lantern light.

At 00:06.000, the goblin lifts his mug a little in thanks as it catches the last drops. Amy steps back a few inches, her hand still on the barrel’s lid, watching the mug fill. The camera pulls out slightly, revealing both subjects side by side against the counter and hearth.
End continuity state: Goblin_1 holds the now‑filled chipped mug at chest height beside the central counter; Amy stands behind him with the barrel beside the hearth; the cloth remains on the floor next to the polished table.

The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. STATIC SETTING AUTHORITY
The tavern hall is a rectangular space measuring thirty feet east‑west by twenty feet north‑south on a creaking wooden floor. The western wall hosts the back door with its wooden latch, centered along that wall. North of the southern wall, ten feet away and centered on the north‑south axis, sits an eight‑by‑six‑foot hearth; in front of it, facing south, is a three‑foot diameter barrel five feet tall. A twelve‑by‑two‑foot counter runs parallel to the western wall just east of the hearth. Four high bar stools sit along this counter at equal intervals: one near each corner and two midway between them. Each stool has a seven‑ft seat height with a one‑by‑one foot platform, and above each sits a six‑by‑two‑foot crystal cup shelf. North of the back door on the western wall is a four‑by‑three‑foot table with an additional one‑foot depth for chairs; two chairs flank this table. Lanterns hang from the ceiling at five feet above floor level over every seating area—four lanterns above each bar stool and one above the back table—to provide continuous illumination without blocking pathways. All objects are positioned to avoid overlap: a four‑ft aisle north of the hearth, the counter east of that space, and ample room between stools for patrons. The layout reflects a medieval tavern interior with wooden fixtures, a hearth, barrels, counters, high stools, crystal cup shelves, tables, chairs, lanterns, and a back door latch.

RAW SCENE
At 00:00.000, Goblin_1 steps onto the counter from the east side of the room.  
At 00:00.125, he leans over the counter with his chin resting on its edge and holds a chipped mug in one hand.  
At 00:00.250, the camera is positioned east of the central counter, a slight angle looking west toward the hearth and back table. The focus is on a green‑skinned goblin leaning over the counter with his chin resting on its edge; he holds a chipped mug in one hand.

Amy stands behind him, her left foot near the counter’s edge, eyes fixed on the barrel beside the hearth. She lifts the ale‑scented cloth from her palm, places it to the side of the polished table, and releases it onto the floor beside the table as she looks at the finish—this is the inherited position from the previous beat.

At 00:01.500, the camera pans left a few feet, pulling both subjects into frame. The goblin’s right hand still grips the mug; Amy’s right arm extends toward the barrel’s lid.

At 00:02.750, Amy lifts her arm and tilts the barrel slightly, tipping liquid out of its side. A stream of ale pours from the barrel onto the chipped mug.

At 00:04.250, the liquid flows steadily; the mug fills up to near its brim, the surface catching the lantern light.

At 00:06.000, the goblin lifts his mug a little in thanks as it catches the last drops. Amy steps back a few inches, her hand still on the barrel’s lid, watching the mug fill. The camera pulls out slightly, revealing both subjects side by side against the counter and hearth.
End continuity state: Goblin_1 holds the now‑filled chipped mug at chest height beside the central counter; Amy stands behind him with the barrel beside the hearth; the cloth remains on the floor next to the polished table.

Amy greets the newcomer with a warm smile and lifts a crystal cup from her shelf, pouring a special brew into it before handing it over.

The dragon licks its lips, then takes a slow sip, letting the liquid swirl across its tongue. Amy watches as the creature exhales contentedly; she wipes the bar with a fresh cloth, smoothing out any drips of ale that have fallen onto the wood.
